So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you 'crashed' after being really fired up - what did you mean by that?\n\nPatient: I just... stopped caring about things. Like, I used to go hiking a lot. Take pictures of creek beds, trees, stuff like that. It used to help clear my head. But now... I don't know. I just leave the camera in the truck. I don't really care if the light is perfect or whatever. Just... lost the point of it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in things like hiking and taking pictures?\n\nPatient: I don't know... a while. It's been hanging over me for about a year, probably. But this... this crash right now... it's the worst it's been.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a period of time where you felt extremely fired up and were working on your bids and park idea non-stop, that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. It started when I was trying to... remake myself, I guess, for the park pitch. It went on for about two weeks. Then I just... crashed.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day, since you crashed?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I don't... I don't really sit around and keep track of it day to day. Just... yeah, I guess I just feel kind of blank. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel extremely happy or on top of the world during those two weeks when you were working on your bids and park idea?\n\nPatient: Not really happy, just... fired up. Like... I don't know, bigger than myself, maybe. But mostly... if a coworker questioned my plan or told me to slow down, I’d just snap at him. Way harder than made sense. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods of time where you felt extremely fired up and irritable that lasted for more than a few days, before you started feeling blank and losing interest in things?\n\nPatient: I thought I just said that. Yeah, for about two weeks I was like that. Before the crash.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any other periods like the two weeks where you were fired up and working on your bids, before that one happened?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I don't really... keep track of things like that. Not that I remember, anyway. Just that one time, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely fired up and irritable like that before the two weeks when you were working on your bids?\n\nPatient: Like I just said... no. I don't think so. Just that one time.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of significantly decreased need for sleep during those two weeks when you were fired up and working on your bids?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... I was just sleeping normally. I didn't really notice anything different with my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt sad or empty for most of the day, for several days in a row, at any point in your life before the past year?\n\nPatient: I don't really... I mean, I don't spend much time thinking about how I felt years ago. I just go to work and do my job, you know? So... no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide since you crashed?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I guess. It's just... I was sitting in my truck after work the other day, and... I don't know. Usually I just wish I wasn't around, you know? Just disappear. But... I got a little scared because I started actually thinking about... how I could do it. With the stuff I had right there in the truck.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling blank and losing interest in things like hiking and taking pictures?\n\nPatient: I don't know exactly. A few months, maybe? Since... since that crash we talked about. I just haven't really felt like doing much of anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling sad or empty since you crashed?\n\nPatient: I guess... maybe a year? It's just kind of been hanging over me for about that long. But... since I crashed recently, it's... it's the worst it's gotten.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt extremely fired up or irritable for several days or more, aside from the two weeks when you were working on your bids?\n\nPatient: No... not that I can think of. Just that one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you enjoyed anything at all since you crashed?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I used to go hiking to clear my head. Take pictures of creek beds, trees, stuff like that. But lately... I just leave the camera in the truck. Even if the light is perfect, I just... I don't care.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, like hiking and taking pictures, for most of the time since you crashed?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. I don't really keep track of the days, but... yeah. Most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Did your crash, where you felt blank and lost interest in things, happen right after you were feeling extremely fired up and working on your bids?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wasn't paying attention to the exact timeline... It just kind of happened.\n\nDoctor: Did your two weeks of being fired up and working on bids interfere with your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, not that I noticed. I was just getting work done. I didn't really pay attention to if it bothered anyone else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
